#2790. Raiders Week 10: Seahawks’ Jaxon Smith-Njigba a supreme challenge

That's the exact amount of times Jaxon Smith-Njigba has been limited to under 100-yards receiving in the nine games the Seattle Seahawks have played this season.

With 71 receptions for a league-leading 1,036 yards and nine touchdowns, the 6-foot, 197-pound 24-year-old is arguably the best wideout in the NFL. To say the Las Vegas Raiders defense will have its hands full with Smith-Njigba in Sunday's Week 10 matchup with the Seahawks this Sunday is an understatement.

Defensive coordinator Rob Leonard knows JSN presents a supreme challenge for Las Vegas and he expects Seattle to exploit any and all matchups.

"Yeah, I mean, he's unbelievable. He's number one in the league across the category in all things, receptions," Leonard said when asked about the collective effort it'll take to limit Smith-Njigba. "But yeah, it's very critical. You start with him day one. We're still talking about him all the way, early down, third down, red zone. Not just coverage-wise, different things that we have to do."

Fully expect the Raiders secondary to get the full JSN experience with cornerbacks Eric Stokes, Darien Porter, Jermod McCoy, Taron Johnson and Hezekiah Masses to get the responsibility of covering the Seahawks elite wide receiver. This is because Seattle isn't shy about deploying their elite wide receiver in both slot and outside alignments. The 20th pick in the 2023 NFL Draft has been a strong slot receiver since he arrived but he's spending more time as a perimeter option, too.

That slot and boundary capability has truly expanded Smith-Njigba's effectiveness and the Ohio State product's production highlights just how much more dangerous he's become as a weapon in the Seahawks offense. And all this despite Seattle's offensive attack not being at the level that current Raiders head coach and play caller Klint Kubiak produced when he was the Seahawks offensive coordinator during the team's Super Bowl-winning run last season.

Rookie offensive coordinator Brian Fleury has Seattle's offense ranked 18th in points scored (202) and yards gained (2,950). The passing game is ranked 17th in yards gained (1,953) and touchdowns scored (15) while the run game is 18th in yards gained (997) and 20th in touchdowns scored (seven). But whether it's Sam Darnold or Drew Lock at quarterback, JSN

Interestingly enough, however, Smith-Njigba's lowest output of the season was last Sunday in a Week 9 tilt with division foe Arizona Cardinals: 10 targets, seven receptions, for 51 yards (long of 18) and no touchdowns. Leonard and the Raiders defense meticulously studying the way the Cardinals covered the Seahawks elite pass catcher in round two of their NFC West contests this year.

And one of the defensive tactics Arizona deployed is something Leonard is keen to do with his Las Vegas defense: Zone-heavy and two-high safety looks.

While Leonard has deployed more man coverage to try and mitigate defensive woes, returning to a zone-heavy look helps Stokes — who excels in Cover 3 and struggles mightily in man-to-man — and allowed Arizona to bracket Smith-Njigba over the top and took away the deep and intermediate routes where Seattle's wideout does a lot of his damage.

Doing this forced working underneath with shorter passes and this is an area where linebackers Nakobe Dean and Quay Walker operate, offering the opportunity to hit JSN — hard. The Cardinals' adjust gameplan severely limited Smith-Njigba and was a far cry from their Week 2 contest where the wideout produced 155 receiving yards and three touchdowns on nine receptions.

To put it plain: The Raiders approach to JSN should be similar to what defenses do when matching up with Las Vegas tight end Brock Bowers — bracket and make other pass catchers beat you.

But what a coverage responsibility and experience Smith-Njigba presents to the younger Raiders cornerbacks like Porter, McCoy and Masses. That trio are 25, 21 and 22 years of age, respectively, and going against one of the game's best wide receivers should be a welcome task, albeit, daunting.

According to Pro Football Reference, Porter is charged with 24 targets allowing 13 receptions (54.2 completion percentage) for 201 yards and one touchdown. Masses has been targeted 31 times allowing 16 receptions (51.6 percent) for 258 yard and a team-leading four touchdowns allowed, while McCoy draws six targets allowing four receptions (66.7 percent) for 63 yards and one touchdown.

"Yeah, he's playing faster. His eyes are in the right spot, and he made a play on the ball, which was awesome," Leonard said when asked what he's seen from McCoy this season. "Him and Zeke (Hezekiah Masses) are both growing, and that's what you're looking for, is improvement, right? So, it's been fun to get him more reps so he can feel a little bit more calm out there."

JSN's elite ability to maintain consistent speed through his routes without telegraphing or slowing down will thoroughly test the entire Raiders secondary's skillset — beyond the cornerbacks, too. Because Seattle will place their elite pass catcher all over the alignment, even in the backfield and alongside an offensive tackle to truly force the opposing defense's hand. And if Dean or Walker draw coverage responsibility based on JSN's alignment and pre-snap motion, good luck, Raiders.

At that point, the pass rush and coverage need to combine so disruption of or taking down the quarterback means no dice for Smith-Njigba.

Sunday's tilt in Allegiant Stadium marks the 57th time the Raiders and Seahawks square off. The Silver & Black lead the all-time series 30-26 (29-25 in regular season play, 1-1 in postseason action). The last time the two teams played one another was in 2022 with Las Vegas dropping Seattle 40-34 in overtime on a walk-off 86-yard run by running back Josh Jacobs. Before that, the Seahawks won the 2018 and 2014 meetings (27-3 and 30-24).

"How aggressive you want to be, based on what their personnel is, what our personnel is, and what gives you the best chance of starting fast. Is it coverage? Is it pressure? Is it a different front? There's a million variables that go into it. And then what personnel package do we want to start with? What 11 do we want to put out there to start the game, and then have a plan to get to a different one later? So those are all factors. How did we practice that week? What was the best thing that we were executing? 'All right, I like that as a lead call.' It's very interactive, both call-wise and personnel-wise, and it's a week-long conversation of that back-and-forth decision of what's best."

—Las Vegas Raiders defensive coordinator Rob Leonard on nuances of starting better defensively

#2789. Top Wall Street analysts prefer these 3 dividend stocks for stable income

Uncertainty in the Middle East, elevated Treasury yields and fears about an AI bubble continue to keep global markets on edge. Given this volatile backdrop, investors seeking stable income can consider enhancing their portfolios by adding dividend-paying stocks.

However, the vast universe of dividend stocks can make selection challenging. Tracking the ratings of top Wall Street analysts can help shortlist attractive dividend stocks, as their recommendations are backed by in-depth research.

Here are three dividend-paying stocks that are highlighted by Wall Street's top pros, as tracked by TipRanks, a platform that ranks analysts based on their past performance.

Chord Energy is an independent exploration and production company with long-lived assets primarily in the Williston Basin. With a quarterly base dividend of $1.30 per share, CHRD offers a dividend yield of 3.67%.

In a research note on third-quarter expectations, RBC Capital analyst Scott Hanold reiterated a buy rating on CHRD stock with a price target of $175. "We model production near the top of guidance and continue to see strong performance from new wells and base production," said Hanold.

Specifically, the five-star analyst expects Q3 2026 production at 283 thousand barrels of oil equivalent per day, or Mboe/d, in line with the Street's consensus and near the high end of the 278 to 284 Mboe/d guidance. Hanold said his Q3 2026 earnings per share estimate increased to $5.29 due to lower non-cash depreciation, depletion and amortization.

Meanwhile, cash flow per share estimate moved lower to $13.59 to reflect Hanold's update based on the current backdrop and commodity mark-to-market. He expects Chord Energy to report capital spending of $375 million for Q3 2026, slightly above the Street's estimate of $369 million.

Overall, Hanold remains bullish on CHRD, given its best-in-class balance sheet. He expects a free cash flow payout of 80% in the third quarter, with the possibility of an upside. Hanold expects investors to focus on management commentary on 2027 capital plans, Williston M&A opportunities, and long-term performance of 4-mile wells.

Hanold ranks No. 310 among more than 12,500 analysts tracked by TipRanks. His ratings have been profitable 63% of the time, delivering an average return of 14.9%. See Chord Energy KPIs on TipRanks.

Williams, an energy infrastructure company that delivers natural gas, is the next dividend pick. The company recently completed the $5.5 billion acquisition of Momentum Midstream, which will expand its natural gas infrastructure in the Haynesville to meet growing Gulf Coast LNG, power and industrial demand. At a quarterly dividend of $0.5250 per share, Williams offers a yield of 2.89%.

In her latest research note, RBC Capital analyst Elvira Scotto reiterated a buy rating on Williams stock with a price target of $87. Ahead of Q3 2026 earnings, the analyst said that she expects WMB to announce strong quarterly performance.

Scotto raised her estimates to reflect insights from the quarterly catch-up call, the completion of the Momentum Midstream acquisition, and mark-to-market quarter-to-date commodity prices. She expects WMB to report Q3 2026 adjusted earnings before interest, taxes, depreciation and amortization of $2,036 million, which is modestly above the Street's consensus estimate.

Furthermore, Scotto expects Williams' Transmission, Power & Gulf segment to increase quarter-over-quarter, driven by the startup of the Socrates power project and favorable natural gas liquids, or NGL, margins.

Scotto noted that WMB stock has underperformed due to backlash related to data centers and investor anticipation of another Power Innovation project. She expects the company to announce at least one more project before the end of this year, potentially with a new customer and a contract duration of 15 to 20 years, which would be a positive catalyst for the stock.

Scotto ranks No. 175 among more than 12,500 analysts tracked by TipRanks. Her ratings have been profitable 67% of the time, delivering an average return of 16.2%. See Williams Ownership Structure on TipRanks.

EOG Resources, a crude oil and natural gas exploration and production company, will hold its third-quarter earnings call on Nov. 6. At a quarterly dividend of $1.02 per share, EOG offers a dividend yield of 2.75%.

Heading into Q3 earnings, Jefferies analyst Lloyd Byrne reiterated a buy rating on EOG Resources stock and increased his price target to $185 from $175. The analyst expects the company's Q3 cash flow per share to exceed the Street's consensus by 13%, driven by better-than-expected production.

Byrne expects crude oil production, excluding the UAE, to reach 551 Mboe/d, which is at the high end of guidance (546-551 Mboe/d) and above the Street's projection of 548 Mboe/d, driven by continued growth in the Utica.

Furthermore, Byrne expects cash operating expenses to come in at $10.27 per barrel of oil equivalent, below the midpoint of guidance of $10.05 to $11.35, helped by lower lease operating expenses. Overall, the analyst expects EOG to deliver free cash flow of $2.65 billion, supporting another quarter of buybacks near the minimum 70% shareholder return threshold and implying about $1.5 billion in share repurchases.

"As a first indication of the '27 plan, we expect EOG will message another year of mid-single-digit (~3.5%) growth on a flat capital budget (~$6.55bn), which is above the Street modeling 1% oil growth at $6.6bn," said Byrne.

Byrne ranks No. 303 among more than 12,500 analysts tracked by TipRanks. His ratings have been successful 58% of the time, delivering an average return of 18.5%. See EOG Resources Statistics on TipRanks.

#2787. Top Wall Street analysts are bullish on the growth potential of these 3 stocks

Fears around the artificial intelligence trade and higher pressure on oil prices are weighing on global markets, but a few stocks offer investors solid growth prospects even in choppy times.

Investors with a long-term horizon can look beyond the ongoing volatility and track the recommendations of top Wall Street analysts to pick attractive stocks with strong long-term growth potential.

Here are three stocks favored by some of Wall Street's top pros, according to TipRanks, a platform that ranks analysts based on their past performance.

Health insurer UnitedHealth delivered better-than-expected results for the third quarter of 2026. The company also raised its full-year earnings outlook, reflecting continued progress on its turnaround plan.

Following the Q3 print, Morgan Stanley analyst Erin Wright reiterated a buy rating on UNH stock with a price target of $529. "UNH's beat & raise reinforces turnaround momentum, with improving MA [Medicare Advantage]/Optum Health supporting a higher '26 EPS baseline & upside to '27 consensus," said Wright.

The five-star analyst added that better-than-expected performance in the UnitedHealthcare and Optum Health businesses supported another upgrade to the company's 2026 adjusted earnings per share outlook to at least $20.50. Wright noted that UNH raised the earnings guidance despite incremental investments planned for the fourth quarter of 2026.

Furthermore, Wright highlighted management's view that the consensus 2027 EPS of $22.63 was too conservative. Reaching even the low end of the company's 13% to 16% long-term EPS growth target would imply an EPS of $23.17.

Wright raised her 2026 EPS estimate to $20.65 from $19.82 and 2027 EPS to $23.24 from $22.54, reflecting the latest upside and an improving 2026 medical loss ratio to 87.4% from 88.1%. Overall, she believes that UNH's operational performance continues to improve, increasing her confidence in its UHC strategy and Optum Health turnaround execution.

Wright ranks No. 264 among more than 12,500 analysts tracked by TipRanks. Her ratings have been successful 64% of the time, delivering an average return of 15%. See UnitedHealth Ownership Structure on TipRanks.

Social media giant Meta Platforms has been making headlines since the launch of its Muse AI agent, which is seeing solid traction. Investors look forward to updates on the company's AI strategy when it announces its third-quarter results on Oct. 21.

Heading into Q3 earnings, Guggenheim analyst Michael Morris maintained a buy rating on Meta stock and increased his price target to $850 from $700, reflecting higher estimates for 2027. He expects Muse usage momentum and engagement levels to shape investor sentiment into the Q3 earnings call.

Given that Muse's current adoption represents less than 0.05% of Meta's 3.6 billion daily active user base, the analyst expects management to prioritize boosting usage over near-term monetization.

"Over time, we believe Muse monetizes primarily through businesses paying to reach consumers through their agents, consistent with the economics of Meta's core advertising business, rather than consumer wallets," said the five-star analyst. He added that the checkout integrations with Shopify and PayPal reflect this framework.

While there are concerns that a rival could overtake Muse or the recently launched Muse Wonder and push Meta into a prolonged cycle of overinvestment, Morris remains bullish due to the company's strong record in scaling WhatsApp and competing strongly with TikTok via Reels as well as free distribution to an extensive user base.

Morris ranks No. 629 among more than 12,500 analysts tracked by TipRanks. His ratings have been successful 57% of the time, delivering an average return of 13.2%. See Meta Platforms KPIs on TipRanks.

After hosting a non-deal roadshow with Marvell's senior management following the Investor Day, Stifel analyst Tore Svanberg reiterated a buy rating on the stock with a price target of $370.

The five-star analyst said that a major focus of the roadshow was the XPU and XPU Attach mix, as Marvell's Custom business expands toward its FY31 revenue target of $30 billion. In this regard, management reiterated that it expects a balanced mix of XPU and XPU Attach revenue in FY31, though it noted that either segment could grow rapidly enough to shift the mix toward 60/40 or 40/60.

While XPU drove most of the nearly $1.5 billion Custom revenue in FY26, Svanberg expects Marvell's XPU Attach business to grow substantially through the end of the decade, leading to a more balanced mix in this business. At the Investor Day event, the company raised its FY29 Custom target from more than $10 billion to over $12 billion, mainly driven by strength in the XPU Attach business.

Svanberg said that another key topic in the roadshow was whether Marvell can secure enough capacity to meet its raised targets. Management said that it met with suppliers ahead of Investor Day to share its five-year goals and received commitments to support these targets. The analyst believes that Marvell's supply chain commitments give it a competitive edge over rivals as customers seek reliable partners for large-scale infrastructure buildouts.

#2786. Cam Skattebo stayed in his stance instead of pushing Jameis Winston's tush on key play

Giants running back Cam Skattebo stayed in his stance and didn’t participate in the play on a key short-yardage snap late in the Giants’ loss on Thursday night.

With the Giants leading 21-17 and 3:56 remaining in the fourth quarter on Thursday night, they faced a third-and-1 and sent Jameis Winston in for a quarterback sneak. A first down would have given the Giants a great chance at a long, clock-draining drive. Instead they were stopped, punted, and ended up losing.

As Winston plowed into the line, Skattebo was behind him and could have run in to try to push him forward to gain a yard. Instead, Skattebo stayed in his stance, and by the time he actually got involved in the play, the Commanders had stopped Winston’s forward progress.

It’s unclear why Skattebo didn’t do anything, and he wasn’t asked about it after the game. But at a time when every team in the league is well aware of the value of teammates pushing the quarterback forward on sneaks, it was a bizarre play, and one that proved costly for the Giants.

#2785. How to watch Warriors vs. Spurs

The Golden State Warriors continue their road trip with Friday night's NBA Cup matchup against the San Antonio Spurs. The game will be played at 6:30 PM PT in San Antonio and can be watched on Prime Video.

Golden State's eight-game winning streak came to an end Wednesday night following a 115-108 loss to the Houston Rockets. However, the result took a backseat to a much bigger concern after superstar Steph Curry suffered an oblique injury in the first quarter that forced him to miss the remainder of the game.

Despite losing their best player early, the Warriors still managed to put up a strong fight against Houston. Rookie Yaxel Lendeborg continued his impressive start to the season, going toe-to-toe with Rockets superstar Kevin Durant at times while leading Golden State with 25 points and 10 rebounds. Brandin Podziemski provided some much-needed scoring support, finishing with 22 points on 7-of-15 shooting from the field, including four three-pointers.

The biggest storyline heading into Friday night is Curry's injury and what it means for Golden State moving forward. The Warriors announced Thursday that their superstar guard suffered an oblique abdominal tear and will be reevaluated in two weeks, leaving the team without its offensive engine for tonight's game and the foreseeable future.

In years past, an extended absence for Curry would have been a devastating blow to Golden State's chances of winning. While that still may be true, the emergence of Lendeborg as a legitimate scoring threat and head coach Steve Kerr's revamped offensive system has this year's Warriors thinking they are much better equipped to weather the storm.

That confidence will be put to the test Friday night against a San Antonio team that enters the matchup with a 10-2 record, the second-best mark in the Western Conference. Wednesday's performance against Houston offered a glimpse of what the Warriors can accomplish without their superstar, but sustaining that level of play will be a tall task, especially against a Spurs team led by reigning Defensive Player of the Year Victor Wembanyama. With Curry sidelined, Golden State will need Lendeborg to build on his impressive rookie campaign, which has seen him average 21.7 points per game on 51.8% shooting from the field, while the team continues its stellar play defensively.

Enjoy the game Dub Nation. GO WARRIORS!!!

Spurs: De'Aaron Fox, Stephon Castle, Dylan Harper, Devin Vassell, Victor Wembanyama

#2784. UCLA QB Falls Helplessly on Ground, Forced to Rely on Nico Iamaleava’s Brother Madden

UCLA had already lost one QB this season. Now the Bruins were watching another leave the field in pain. Ty Dieffenbach went down late in the second quarter against Illinois after taking a hard hit while trying to escape pressure. He landed awkwardly on his left shoulder, stayed on the turf for several minutes, and eventually made his way toward the sideline.

Bruins Report Online relayed the television update on X, writing, "The Fox telecast is reporting it is a shoulder injury for Ty Dieffenbach."

The incident happened with just 35 seconds left before halftime. Ty Dieffenbach scrambled to avoid the Illinois rush before defender Carter Janki caught him around the legs. The hit sent him crashing down, with his left shoulder taking the brunt of the landing. He struggled to get up and was eventually helped off the field. The Fox broadcast listed him as questionable to return.

There wasn't much time for UCLA to process the situation. Madden Iamaleava, Nico's younger brother, was suddenly called upon to run the offense. The sophomore entered with almost no game experience. He had attempted just one pass all season before being thrown into a game with considerably more at stake.

But Madden wasn't exactly an unproven talent. The former four-star recruit threw for 3,626 yards, 43 touchdowns, and just four interceptions during his junior season at Warren High School. ESPN ranked him No. 142 nationally in the 2025 recruiting class before he started his college career at Arkansas and transferred to UCLA. However, he had barely played at the college level, appearing in just one game in 2025 without attempting a pass. Now, the Bruins were asking him to deliver under pressure with their undefeated season at stake.

UCLA head coach Bob Chesney didn't sound very concerned about giving him the opportunity. Speaking with the team's radio broadcast while heading off the field, he offered a brief reaction.

"I'm excited to see what Madden can do in there," he said.

Ty Dieffenbach wasn't visible on the Bruins' sideline early in the third quarter, and Madden took over under center to begin the second half. UCLA carried a 10-0 advantage into the break, but the QB situation had become something the Bruins knew all too well.

Nico Iamaleava, their original starter, suffered a season-ending ACL injury after five games during UCLA's upset victory over Oregon last month. His absence opened the door for Ty Dieffenbach, who transferred from Cal Poly and took over the starting job.

He made the most of it. Ty Dieffenbach won all four of his starts, helping UCLA maintain an unbeaten record. He had thrown seven touchdowns against three interceptions this season, giving Bob Chesney a dependable replacement after losing his first-choice QB.

Against Illinois, Ty Dieffenbach had already thrown for 168 yards and a touchdown before the injury interrupted his night. Now the Bruins were asking Madden to keep things moving. That's a huge jump for a QB who had barely seen the field.

At 9-0, UCLA is enjoying its best start since 1998. The Bruins have also reached nine wins for the first time since 2022 and are chasing a regular-season milestone the program hasn't achieved in 28 years. But the QB injuries are piling up at an uncomfortable time.

And the stakes couldn't have been much higher. UCLA entered the Illinois matchup undefeated at 9-0, sitting at No. 4 in the College Football Playoff rankings. The Bruins were also leading the Big Ten at 6-0, with a chance to clinch a conference championship berth if they beat Illinois and five other conference contenders lost. With a playoff spot and a potential Big Ten title on the line, losing another starting quarterback was the last thing UCLA needed.

The 10-0 halftime lead offers some breathing room, but it doesn't solve the bigger problem. With Ty Dieffenbach unavailable, at least temporarily, UCLA will need its defense and running game, led by Wayne Knight, to carry more of the load.

UCLA had already survived losing Nico Iamaleava. Now it had to see whether his brother could help prevent another QB injury from derailing an otherwise remarkable season.

#2783. Vikings-Packers Final Injury Report

The Minnesota Vikings and Green Bay Packers released their final injury reports, along with injury designations, in advance of their matchup on Sunday at Lambeau Field. Here they are:

PUP = Physically unable to participate list.

IR = Injured reserve. Note: only starters on IR are listed.

Probable = the league defines a Probable injury designation as a 75% or greater chance of playing. Historically, 95% of players designated probable are active and played on game day.

Questionable = the league defines a Questionable injury designation as being 'uncertain' of whether the player will play or not. This replaces the former definition of having a 50% chance of playing. Historically, 70-75% of players with a Questionable injury designation were active and played on game day.

Doubtful = The league defines a Doubtful injury designation to mean the player is unlikely to participate in the game. In practical terms, teams use the designation when a player has less than a 50% chance to suit up, and active game appearances from this designation remain rare. Historically only around 6% of players with a Doubtful designation are active and played on game day.

Out = will not play. Players with this designation are not active on game day and league rules prohibit them from playing.

For the Vikings, kind of the worst-case scenario based on the injury list earlier this week, with only Kyle Van Noy being upgraded today among previous DNP players- the rest of which are out on Sunday, including Blake Cashman, T.J. Hockenson, Tyrion Ingram-Dawkins, Jauan Jennings, and Kyler Murray. Moreover, Jordan Addison and Josh Metellus were added to the injury report as both were limited on Friday and carry questionable injury designations. Hopefully both will be able to go on Sunday, however. Not having Hockenson will make it a bit tougher for the debut of Kirk Cousins on Sunday while not having Blake Cashman means Eric Wilson may have the green dot on defense on Sunday with a greater role for Ivan Pace Jr. and probably Jake Golday as well.

For the Packers, starters Josh Jacobs, Sean Rhyan, and Micah Parsons are out, as expected. Starting linebacker Zaire Franklin is doubtful, meaning he probably won't play on Sunday. A few other starters are questionable for the Packers on Sunday, including right guard Kevin Zeitler, defensive end Lukas Van Ness, and safeties Javon Bullard and Xavier McKinney- who was added to the injury list on Friday with a foot injury. Lastly, running back Chris Brooks is also questionable, and with Josh Jacobs out on Sunday may be expected to have more of a role if he's able to go. But while I expect most of the questionable players to play on Sunday for the Packers, most of them have injuries that may keep them at less than 100%.

Overall, both teams are a bit banged up and will be missing some key starters on Sunday.

#2782. Top Wall Street analysts prefer these 3 dividend stocks for stable income

Global stock markets remain volatile amid persistent geopolitical tensions and concerns about the sustainability of AI trade. Given this backdrop, investors seeking steady income can add dividend-paying stocks to their portfolios.

Tracking recommendations from top Wall Street analysts can help investors pick attractive stocks, as these experts assess a company's ability to pay dividends consistently.

Here are three dividend-paying stocks that are highlighted by Wall Street's top pros, as tracked by TipRanks, a platform that ranks analysts based on their past performance.

Integrated oil and gas giant Chevron (CVX) is a dividend king. The company has increased its dividend for 39 consecutive years. At a quarterly dividend of $1.78 per share (annualized dividend of $7.12 per share), CVX offers a yield of 3.14%. Chevron will announce its third-quarter results on October 30.

In an earnings preview note on U.S. oil majors, Goldman Sachs analyst Neil Mehta reiterated a buy rating on Chevron with a price target of $241. The analyst is focused on the company's international growth prospects across Latin America, the Middle East, West Africa, and the Eastern Mediterranean.

Specifically, Mehta highlighted management's commentary on solid execution in Venezuela, with gross production expected to more than double to nearly 600 kbd (kilo barrels per day) by 2031 via three joint ventures. He also noted a recent oil and gas condensate discovery in offshore Angola and is awaiting commercial updates on the West Qurna 2 and Nasiriyah projects in Iraq.

Furthermore, Mehta expects CVX to report improved productivity and capital efficiency across domestic shale assets. He expects the improvement mainly in the Permian Basin, where Chevron projects advanced chemical treatments to drive about a 10% recovery uplift in new wells and bring down capital spend per barrel by about 25% year-over-year. Overall, Mehta expects Chevron to report Q3 2026 EPS of $4.95, above the consensus of $4.89.

"While unfavorable timing effects and volatile commodity prices may present quarterly earnings headwinds, we highlight healthy U.S. refining margins and strong asset utilization as partial offsets," said Mehta.

Mehta ranks No. 381 among more than 12,500 analysts tracked by TipRanks. His ratings have been profitable 63% of the time, delivering an average return of 12%. See Chevron KPIs on TipRanks.

Kinetik Holdings (KNTK) is a fully integrated, pure-play, midstream company operating in the Delaware Basin. Earlier this month, the company declared a dividend of $0.81 per share, payable on October 30, 2026. With an annualized dividend of $3.24 per share, KNTK offers a yield of 5.82%. Kinetik will announce its Q3 earnings on November 4, with a conference call scheduled the next day.

Ahead of Q3 2026 earnings, Goldman Sachs analyst John Mackay reiterated a buy rating on Kinetik stock and slightly raised his price target to $58 from $57. He expects the company to report Q3 2026 EBITDA of $279 million, higher than the Street's expectation of $270 million but lower than the second quarter's EBITDA of $281 million.

The 5-star analyst expects Kinetik's Q3 performance to gain from higher gas volumes after notable curtailments in the second quarter. Mackay also expects the quarter to benefit from higher production and steady commodity margins, even though some lower-margin contracts and weaker Waha spread marketing gains could weigh on results.

Overall, Mackay increased his Q3 2026 estimates to reflect higher volumes, driven by rapid Permian egress pipeline commissioning, stronger throughput at Kings Landing I, and higher M2M commodity prices, offset by increased costs.

"As natural gas takeaway constraints ease across the Permian, investor focus centers on KNTK's YE26 volume trajectory and durability of its multi-year growth," said Mackay.

Mackay ranks No. 569 among more than 12,500 analysts tracked by TipRanks. His ratings have been profitable 64% of the time, delivering an average return of 16.9%. See Kinetik's Ownership Structure on TipRanks.

Moving to Crescent Energy (CRGY), an exploration and production company with activities focused in the Eagle Ford, Permian, and Uinta Basins. With a quarterly dividend of $0.12 per share (annualized dividend of $0.48 per share), CRGY's dividend yield stands at 3.56%. Earlier this month, Crescent announced an agreement to acquire Eagle Ford assets from Devon Energy (DVN).

Heading into Q3 earnings on Nov. 2, Evercore analyst Stephen Richardson reiterated a buy rating on Crescent stock and slightly lowered the price target to $17 from $18, citing dilution from new shares.

"CRGY's recent acquisition of DVN's Eagle Ford assets marks a strategic, growth-oriented move, potentially enhancing cash flow performance," said Richardson.

The 5-star analyst noted that the acquisition of the Eagle Ford assets at a net purchase price of $3.85 billion will add about 68 MBoe/d of net production and over 600 net Tier 1 locations. Richardson added that a solid track record of synergies from past deals in the Eagle Ford region increases confidence about Crescent achieving efficiencies with this deal.

Additionally, Richardson noted that the financial implications of the acquisition seem impressive, with a projected increase in cash flow per share of about 9% for 2027 to 2029.

The analyst believes that Crescent is not just expanding its business through acquisitions but positioning itself as a more durable company, regardless of market conditions.

Richardson ranks No. 478 among more than 12,500 analysts tracked by TipRanks. His ratings have been profitable 63% of the time, delivering an average return of 13.2%. See Crescent Energy Statistics on TipRanks.

#2780. Robinhood shares fall as crypto slump weighs on profits, offsetting gains in new businesses

Robinhood shares were down around 8% in pre-market trading on Wednesday as investors digested weaker-than-expected third-quarter earnings despite growth in options trading and prediction markets.

Here's how the brokerage performed in its quarter ended Sept. 30, compared with Wall Street estimates, based on a survey of analysts by LSEG:

Robinhood's net income fell to $468 million, or 51 cents per share, from $556 million, or 61 cents per share, a year ago. Revenue represented a 12% increase from $1.27 billion in the same quarter last year.

Despite that gain, transaction-based revenue of $867 million came in below Wall Street estimates from StreetAccount of $879 million, putting focus on whether Robinhood can sustain momentum across its trading businesses and reduce its reliance on volatile market activity.

Robinhood has been diversifying its business as it tries to expand its brokerage app into a broader financial services platform. Options trading and event contracts have become increasingly important sources of transaction revenue, while the company has continued to invest in wealth management, subscriptions and other offerings.

Transaction-based revenue grew 19% from the same period last year to $867 million, driven primarily by a more than 500% increase in event contracts revenue. Additionally, options revenue grew 31% to $399 million and equities revenue rose 41% to $121 million.

Those gains were partially offset by cryptocurrency revenue, which fell 55% to $120 million.

Chief Financial Officer Shiv Verma told CNBC event contracts on crypto movements did well in the quarter, offering investors another way to express a view on crypto despite the slump in prices and spot trading.

Net deposits on the Robinhood platform totaled $15.7 billion for the quarter, equal to a 17% annualized growth rate based on total platform assets at the end of Q2 2026. Over the past year, net deposits totaled $71 billion, representing 21% growth compared with total platform assets at the end of Q3 2025.

—With reporting from CNBC's Robert Hum and Kate Rooney

#2777. Western Beaver knocks off Steel Valley in WPIAL 2A football quarterfinals

The Western Beaver head coach has done a song and dance with Steel Valley the last four years of his coaching tenure. For the past three years, the Ironmen have sent the Golden Bears packing from the WPIAL Class 2A playoffs.

Busby and his Golden Beavers snagged an 8-6 victory at Fox Chapel High School against their longtime foe. The win brought smiles upon all Western Beaver coaches, players and fans alike.

"A lot of history in this rivalry," Busby said. "It feels really good. The last two or three times, you think you've got it in the bag. But then things happen. Our kids have been resilient all year. They believed in each other, and they've worked. They have each other's backs. Tonight's just one more example in that story."

It was a tale of defenses in the quarterfinal matchup. After a scoreless first quarter, the Golden Beavers got on the board thanks to a 15-yard touchdown from quarterback Jeremiah Pratt.

The junior also converted the two-point conversion attempt. Pratt finished the contest with 123 yards on 17 carries.

"My line made that happen," he said. "I just try to remind those guys to give us an opening. One play is all that we need."

Western Beaver held off any offensive attempts from the Ironmen in the third quarter. However, the Golden Bears began to falter in the fourth quarter, turning the ball over twice.

Steel Valley capitalized on the first turnover, chipping away six minutes off the clock and getting quarterback Gianni Keyes into the end zone. In order to tie, the Ironmen went for the two-point conversion.

Required Reading: Beaver Valley high school football WPIAL quarterfinal scores

Required Reading: Hopewell survives upset scare from North Catholic to advance to WPIAL semifinals

Required Reading: WPIAL high school football playoffs Beaver Valley predictions

Instead of tying the game, Keyes found himself in the arms of linebacker Braeden Mayo.

Mayo said he went into the play with "full confidence."

"The whole game I was timing up the snap," the senior said. "I just said, you know, screw it, I'm going to time that up again and go. I was able to make the play."

Pratt said that he had no doubt his defense would get it done on the two-point conversion.

"I'm standing on the sidelines and I just got hurt," he said. "I'm thinking, 'I can get back in there' if they don't get that stop. I thought, if they score, I'm going back in. That was the biggest play of the game."

"We knew that there was a chance that it was going to come down to our defense and making a stand," Busby said. "We talked about that at halftime. We knew that once we got the two, that if they scored, there would a two-point try from them at some point. We decided the defense that we wanted if we got in that situation. It came out just the way we wanted."

The Golden Beavers coach added that Pratt and Mayo both played like "warriors," fighting through second half injuries to remain in the game and make big plays.

"They didn't want to come off the field," he said. "They were limited in what they were able to do, but they weren't going to leave the field in this game, not with how tight it was. They worked hard to get that victory with their brothers."

Busby said to his team postgame that he's ready for another week of training and preparation as the Golden Beavers advance to the Class 2A semifinals. They'll face off with No. 1 seeded Bishop Canevin, which defeated Beaver Falls 49-12 at Dormont Stadium, next Friday.

"We're playing with house money at this point," Busby said. "There's a lot of love in our locker room. These guys care about each other and their coaches. We're just happy to spend another week together. These guys are resilient – in the way they support each other and in the way they battle."

Mayo said that he and his 'brothers' are looking forward to a battle against the top seed.

"We need to be ready to work next week," he said. "It's a big game. Bishop's not going to lay down. They're a really good team, but I think we can pull it through."

#2776. Hugs will wait as Raiders’ ex-Seahawks trio hopes to spoil reunion with former team

For all the time we spend talking about who needs a ring, who deserves a ring, and whose perfectly fine career will forever be held against him because he never won a ring, you'd think a guy who finally got his hands on one would wear the thing to the grocery store.

Andrew Janocko has worn his two times. And only at the urging of his better half.

"I wore it this summer twice at the request of my wife," the Raiders' offensive coordinator said Thursday.

Las Vegas Raiders head coach Klint Kubiak, middle, reacts with assistant coaches during an NFL football game in Santa Clara, Calif., Sunday, Nov. 8, 2026. AP Photo/Justine Willard

The rest of the time, the Super Bowl ring Janocko earned as the Seahawks' quarterbacks coach stays tucked away in a safe deposit box. Which tells you something about how little time the NFL gives coaches to enjoy the very thing they spend their professional lives chasing.

Consider Janocko's boss. On a special Sunday in February, Klint Kubiak called the plays that helped Seattle beat New England for the Super Bowl championsip. The very next day, the Raiders were announcing him as their head coach.

Many of us have had weekends off that lasted far longer than Kubiak got to be the reigning Super Bowl champion offensive coordinator of the Seattle Seahawks.

Kubiak wasted little time bringing two of his Super Bowl lieutenants with him to the desert. He tapped Janocko, who coached Sam Darnold in Seattle, to run the Raiders' offense and Rick Dennison, who coordinated the Seahawks' run game, to coach the offensive line.

Seattle Seahawks head coach Mike Macdonald is doused with Gatorade during Super Bowl LX against the New England Patriots, Sunday, Feb. 8, 2026, in Santa Clara, Calif. Charles Wenzelberg/New York Post

Fernando Mendoza's first NFL start will command most of the attention at Allegiant Stadium, and rightfully so. But consider what the afternoon asks of three men on the Raiders' sideline. They spent last season figuring out how to get a special group of Seahawks to the Super Bowl. They've spent this week trying to make sure their former colleagues have a miserable flight home.

"It's a big one because it's the next one," Janocko said of Sunday's game.

Seattle Seahawks head coach Mike Macdonald watches his players warm up prior to an NFL football game against the Arizona Cardinals in Seattle, Sunday, Nov. 8, 2026. AP Photo/Lindsey Wasson

Give him credit. That might be the only way to make facing the team you won a championship with sound like a Thursday night in Cleveland.

Kubiak had a pretty good reason for holding off on the nostalgia himself. When the subject came up early in the week, he still had a 38–17 loss to the 49ers to sort through. There were five Raiders turnovers on that film. The trip down memory lane could wait until after the walkthrough.

"And I'll turn the page here later this afternoon," he said.

Ask Kubiak what he brought with him from Seattle, though, and he'll give you something to chew on. Tom Brady and the Raiders saw what he did with Darnold and wanted him to develop the quarterback they would select first overall. Kubiak, for his part, was paying attention to how the two men above him, head coach Mike Macdonald and general manager John Schneider, ran a football team.

Fernando Mendoza of the Las Vegas Raiders warms up before a game against the San Francisco 49ers in Santa Clara, California, Sunday, Nov. 8, 2026. Getty Images

"Number one, I think it's just organizationally, just an all-football building, and from John Schneider to Coach Macdonald, just extremely intelligent people that treat people the right way and get the most out of their people because they're genuinely good people."

That might be the piece of Seattle Kubiak can put to work fastest. On Sunday, Mendoza will come off the field after his first series as an NFL starter and look for somebody to talk to. He'll have the head coach who called plays for a Super Bowl winner and the coordinator who sat next to that quarterback between possessions. Whatever the kid just saw, there's a decent chance one of them has seen it across a practice field.

As for what happens when Janocko sees the people he shared all that with, he's made room in the schedule.

"We'll hug and stuff after the game, but we'll let everyone else deal with that."

There'll be time for a handshake, a hug and, if the Raiders can pull this off, maybe a little grief for a few old friends. Anybody hoping to get a look at Janocko's ring, though, ought to take it up with his wife. So far, she's the only one who's gotten him to wear it.

#2775. Top Wall Street analysts are bullish on these 3 stocks for the long haul

The ongoing earnings season, Middle East tensions and buzz ahead of the U.S. midterm elections have been keeping investors busy. Further, worries about elevated artificial intelligence spending are weighing on investor sentiment.

Investors looking beyond the short-term noise and focusing on long-term returns can track top Wall Street analysts to pick the right stocks, backed by strong research.

Here are three stocks favored by some of Wall Street's top pros, according to TipRanks, a platform that ranks analysts based on their past performance.

This week's first stock pick is Datadog, an AI-powered observability and security platform. The company will announce its results for the third quarter on Nov. 4. Datadog is seeing strong demand for its offerings, thanks to cloud migration and rapid AI adoption. TipRanks' KPIs tool for DDOG shows the growth in its customer base over the past few years amid increasing market penetration and growing adoption of its products.

Heading into Q3 FY26 earnings, Bank of America analyst Koji Ikeda reiterated a buy rating on Datadog stock and raised his price target to $335 from $305. The analyst noted that industry checks and data indicate that infrastructure software demand is strengthening, with AI increasingly driving usage and growth.

Ikeda called Datadog and Atlassian his top picks, saying that companies that can show that AI is a tailwind to growth could see their valuation rerate, even at premium multiples. He added that the total addressable market for observability is growing faster than anticipated, driven by accelerated growth of enterprise AI and the rapid ramp of consumer AI agents.

The five-star analyst believes that focus will be on revenue growth, which he estimates to be 28.7% for Q3 FY26 versus the Street's consensus of 29.1%. Ikeda thinks that the market is underestimating the growth prospects of DDOG's security products. Meanwhile, he cautioned investors about concentration risk related to large customers.

"We think results will be supportive of our long-term thesis that Datadog is a strong share gainer in the rapidly expanding observability opportunity," said Ikeda.

Ikeda ranks No. 673 among more than 12,500 analysts tracked by TipRanks. His ratings have been successful 55% of the time, delivering an average return of 10.9%. See Datadog Statistics on TipRanks.

The next pick, semiconductor giant Nvidia, is also benefiting from AI-driven demand for its products. After attending Nvidia's GTC Berlin conference, Morgan Stanley analyst Joseph Moore reaffirmed a buy rating on NVDA stock with a price target of $300, calling it a top pick in the semiconductor space.

The five-star analyst said that the conference supported his thesis that Nvidia continues to effectively navigate bottlenecks in the supply chain, land/power/shell, and funding. In fact, Moore is "convinced that all 3 aspects are opportunities for NVDA."

While Nvidia's GTC Berlin offered no major updates, Moore noted the conference reflected that the company continues to add to its significant advantages heading into 2027. The analyst said that Vera Rubin is gaining momentum, though details on next year's Rubin Ultra were lighter than expected.

With demand outpacing supply, Moore said that focus is on closing the gap. In this regard, he highlighted the supply chain deals on substrate, packaging and testing. While the analyst continues to view TSMC 3 nm wafer supply as the biggest challenge, he expects it to ease over time.

#2773. Top Wall Street analysts like these 3 dividend stocks for consistent income

Democrats' midterm election win has put the artificial intelligence trade in question, as voters clearly expressed opposition to the rapid buildout of data centers. The stock market could remain volatile amid AI trade concerns, macro uncertainties, and geopolitical tensions.

In this scenario, investors can add dividend-paying stocks to their portfolios to earn steady income. However, the extensive universe of dividend stocks can make selection challenging. Top Wall Street analysts' recommendations can help investors shortlist attractive dividend stocks, as these experts assign ratings after in-depth financial analysis.

Here are three dividend-paying stocks that are highlighted by Wall Street's top pros, as tracked by TipRanks, a platform that ranks analysts based on their past performance.

Viper Energy (VNOM) is a subsidiary of Diamondback and owns and acquires mineral and royalty interests, primarily in the Permian Basin. The company reported upbeat third-quarter earnings, supported by recent acquisitions and continued development activity from Diamondback and third-party operators. Viper announced a base cash dividend of 50 cents per share for Q3 2026, payable on Nov. 19. Based on the base and variable dividends announced over the past 12 months, VNOM's dividend yield stands at 5.7%.

Following the Q3 results, TD Cowen analyst Aaron Bilkoski reiterated a buy rating on Viper Energy stock with a price target of $61. The analyst noted that the company delivered solid Q3 results, with production, including oil and natural gas liquids, exceeding both TD Cowen and consensus estimates.

The 5-star analyst highlighted that the key update was the announcement of two royalty acquisitions for $809 million in cash, including deferred consideration, and $82 million of newly issued shares.

Given the recently announced base dividend hike, Bilkoski expects Viper to direct incremental free cash flow toward buybacks. While the acquisitions increase leverage, the analyst believes that Viper has enough capacity for opportunistic buybacks.

Bilkoski noted that Viper has historically delivered one of the highest production per share growth profiles in his royalty coverage, but still trades at a notable discount to the royalty peer average and the company's historical FCF yield range. "In our view, its above-average growth warrants a higher valuation," said the analyst.

Bilkoski ranks No. 525 among more than 12,500 analysts tracked by TipRanks. His ratings have been profitable 57% of the time, delivering an average return of 12.80%. See Viper Energy Stock Buybacks on TipRanks.

Exxon Mobil (XOM) reported robust Q3 2026 profits, as its U.S. refineries boosted production to capitalize on the high diesel fuel prices sparked by the conflict in the Middle East. The oil and gas giant announced a quarterly dividend of $1.07 per share, payable on Dec. 10, reflecting a 3.9% increase. This marks Exxon's 44th consecutive year of dividend increases. At an annualized dividend of $4.28 per share, XOM offers a yield of 2.53%.

Impressed by the Q3 print, TD Cowen analyst Jason Gabelman reiterated a buy rating on Exxon stock and raised his price target to $193 from $180 to reflect higher downstream earnings, with the expectation of increased mid-cycle margins.

Gabelman noted that the strength in Exxon's refining business continues to offset Middle East-related headwinds. He added that the company increased its ownership in LNG projects, which should reduce its Middle East concentration over time from 75% to 50% and lower the likelihood of diversification-focused mergers and acquisitions. Beyond LNG, Gabelman expects Exxon's growth in the 2030s to come from Guyana, Kashagan, Permian, and possibly Venezuela.

Gabelman noted that Exxon announced a dividend hike of nearly 4% and plans to maintain buybacks at $20 billion for 2027. "The decision to maintain reduces the pro-cyclicality nature of the buyback," said the analyst.

Gabelman ranks No. 347 among more than 12,500 analysts tracked by TipRanks. His ratings have been successful 66% of the time, delivering an average return of 16.90%. See Exxon Mobil Ownership Structure on TipRanks.

Williams (WMB), which owns and operates energy infrastructure to deliver natural gas, reported strong third-quarter results. The company announced a quarterly dividend of $0.525 per share, or $2.10 per share annualized, payable on Dec. 28. WMB offers a dividend yield of 3.09%.

In reaction to the Q3 2026 results, CIBC analyst Robert Catellier reaffirmed a buy rating on Williams stock with a price target of $82. The analyst noted that Q3 results were essentially in line with expectations and reflected continued strength in execution across WMB's infrastructure portfolio.

Catellier said that Williams' Q3 performance was driven by higher transmission, storage, and power generation revenues, as well as contributions from the Momentum Midstream acquisition.

He noted that Williams maintained its full-year EBITDA guidance of $8.3 billion to $8.5 billion. In fact, results are tracking toward the higher end of the guidance range, backed by contributions from recently completed projects and the Momentum Midstream deal. WMB continues to target long-term EBITDA compound annual growth rate of at least 11% through 2030.

Catellier expects WMB's long-term targets to be supported by incremental transmission and storage opportunities, including Transco's Southeast Supply Enhancement II project and an 84 MMcf/d expansion of the Garden Connector project.

#2771. Morgan Scalley's Utah Flight Gets Denied Hours Before Rivalry Game Despite Airport Ground Stops Lifting

The No. 2 Utah Utes hit a massive unexpected travel roadblock right before one of the biggest rivalry games of their season. On Friday night, head coach Morgan Scalley and his team were all packed up and ready to fly from Salt Lake City down to Tucson, Arizona, for their huge Big 12 showdown against the Arizona Wildcats!

However, as national college football writer David Ubben reported on X, Utah's charter flight got completely shut down at the last minute, leaving the entire program stranded in Utah overnight when they were supposed to be settling into their hotel rooms.

The root of the whole travel nightmare was a rare, four-hour nationwide ground stop issued by the Federal Aviation Administration (FAA) on Friday afternoon. Air Route Traffic Control Centers across the country experienced serious communication glitches, forcing aviation officials to freeze takeoffs nationwide for safety reasons.

Because commercial and charter flights were stuck sitting on tarmacs and at gates for hours on end, airline schedules across the United States went off the rails, creating a massive ripple effect just as college football teams were trying to head out for their weekend road games.

Even after the FAA finally resolved the technical issues and lifted the ground stop on Friday evening, Utah's bad luck wasn't over. When the Utes tried to get their flight moving again, their charter airline broke the frustrating news: every single pilot available to fly them had officially "timed out" for the day.

Under strict federal aviation safety rules, pilots are only legally allowed to be on duty for a certain number of hours before they must take a mandatory rest period, and sitting around waiting for the four-hour ground stop to lift ate up all of their remaining clock.

With zero legal crew members left to operate the plane, the flight simply couldn't happen. Suddenly facing a logistical crisis, Coach Scalley and Utah's athletic department had to make a quick call on how to get a roster of over 100 players, coaches, and staff down to Tucson.

Driving wasn't really a smart option since a charter bus trip from Salt Lake City to Tucson takes roughly 12.5 hours, which would have wrecked the players' sleep and left them stiff and exhausted on game day.

So, the team packed back up, headed back to the Eccles Football Center on campus in Salt Lake City for a good night's sleep in their own beds, and pivoted to a same-day flight plan for Saturday morning.

Because same-day travel in college football is super rare and throws off a team's entire pregame routine, the Big 12 Conference had to step in and adjust the schedule. League officials announced that Saturday's kickoff at Casino Del Sol Stadium in Tucson would be pushed back by three full hours, moving from 5:30 p.m. MST to a late-night 8:30 p.m. MST start.

That extra three-hour cushion gives the Utes plenty of breathing room to fly down on Saturday morning, get checked into their day-room hotel, eat their pregame meals, get taped up, and properly warm up without feeling rushed.

It turns out Utah wasn't the only team caught in this chaotic travel mess, either!

Other Big 12 programs like Kansas State and Arizona State also had their Friday flights wrecked by the FAA shutdown and saw their Saturday kickoff times pushed back. Even in the SEC, programs like Ole Miss ran into the exact same issue with their pilots timing out on the tarmac.

Now, all eyes are on how both teams handle the late-night adjustment when they finally hit the field. Utah is fighting to protect its No. 2 national ranking and keep its Big 12 title and playoff hopes alive, while Arizona gets to host a hyped-up "after dark" desert crowd that will have three extra hours to tailgate!

#2769. Tokenization platform Securitize gains 10% postmarket on Q3 results, move into asset-backed finance

Tokenization platform Securitize climbed about 10% postmarket after reporting third quarter growth alongside increased operating expenses, and announcing an expansion into asset-backed finance through a strategic acquisition.

Miami-based Securitize, which made its public market debut in July, operates in the relatively new corner of the crypto equity universe involved in institutional tokenization infrastructure. Tokenized assets on the platform grew 9% in the third quarter, to $4.8 billion, from the same period a year ago. Aggregate transaction volume rose 20% from a year ago, to $4.3 billion. Revenue of $11.5 million was up 28% from last year.

The growth came as bullish sentiment around tokenization gained traction throughout the third quarter, ahead of the Security and Exchange Commission formalizing the long awaited Innovation Exemption on Sept. 17, which created a temporary pathway for trading of certain tokenized stocks and strengthened the regulatory outlook.

Profitability is still meaningfully below year-ago levels, due in part to costs associated with going public over the summer, as well as the scaling up of the tokenization business at an active time for the entire sector, Securitize CEO Carlos Domingo told CNBC in an exclusive interview.

Securitize reinforced "certain areas of the company like finance, compliance, legal [and] cybersecurity," which increased corporate costs "compared to a year ago when we were privately held," Domingo said. "We're in in a very good position. The adjusted EBITDA … is improving significantly from previous quarters."

Securitize reported an adjusted EBITDA loss of $2 million, compared with positive adjusted EBITDA of $3.6 million a year ago, better than a $5.5 million loss in the second quarter. The company posted a 183% increase in operating costs and expenses.

Also on Monday, the Securitize board authorized a $50 million share buyback and announced the acquisition of River One, an institutional asset-backed financing platform developing technology to connect stablecoin capital with traditional lending markets. Terms were not disclosed.

Securitize sees an opportunity to modernize the $9 trillion market for asset backed finance by using stablecoins to finance real world assets, generate yield and tokenize investment positions and credit products, Domingo said.The acquisition also marks an expansion beyond Securitize's core niche markets at a time when tokenization's competitive landscape is growing, with traditional asset managers like BlackRock and Franklin Templeton, and established financial market infrastructure providers like DTCC and Broadridge, entering the industry.

"We have been very active in tokenized funds, treasuries [and] yield … And we've been obviously recently very active in tokenized stocks, so this adds another piece of the puzzle in the emerging tokenization space for us to capture revenue growth."

#2765. What's ahead for AI financing after Nvidia CEO Jensen Huang departs

Nvidia CEO Jensen Huang's departure hasn't produced a broad shock to the credit market, but it is putting the company's role as a financier of artificial intelligence infrastructure under scrutiny.

Huang, who stepped down Thursday amid an espionage and adultery scandal, built Nvidia into an AI powerhouse in the 33 years since he co-founded the company. Chief financial officer Colette Kress has been named interim CEO as the company searches for a permanent chief executive.

Nvidia not only supplies its graphics processing units to hyperscalers and tech giants, it also provides financing to other companies to help the industry expand. That could all change.

"Although we expect the company to fulfill all of its previously announced commitments, we believe that going forward, it may be more difficult for the company to negotiate new multi-party investments among ecosystem partners at the same scale it has done up to this point," Goldman Sachs analyst James Schneider said in a note Thursday.

The news of Huang's resignation comes after investors were already concerned about the massive amount of debt being taken on to fund AI infrastructure.

Tech bond yields have risen and spreads have widened on credit default swaps, most notably in lower-rated names. Credit default swaps are contracts in which investors pay for protection in case the borrower can't repay its debt. Wider spreads indicate investors view the bonds as riskier.

On Friday, the yield on Nvidia's 4.5% bond due in 2031 sat at 5.717%, nearly 5% higher week to date. Bond yields move inversely to prices.

Tech stocks slumped Friday with the Nasdaq 100 closing down 1.2% as markets continued to digest the Nvidia news. Yields on hyperscaler bonds ticked slightly higher on Friday, signaling the broader concern about AI debt lingers.

It's the smaller AI companies that could ultimately see an impact if Nvidia makes any changes to its AI financing. The company had $36 billion in off-balance-sheet AI cloud agreements, or backstops, for neoclouds, according to its last quarterly financial filing in July.

Neoclouds CoreWeave, Nebius and IREN are all in Nvidia's portfolio. Their shares were down sharply Thursday.

CoreWeave, which has become the poster child for AI debt fears, saw its bond yields retreat after its quarterly earnings on Tuesday eased investor concerns. The yield on its 9.75% bond due in 2031 was 14.79% on Friday after climbing to 16.7% last week. The spread on its five-year CDS also dropped more than 17% week to date, although it remained above 1,000 — which is generally considered a sign of distress.

It remains unclear how Nvidia's support for those companies may change, said CreditSights analyst Andy Li.

"We don't know what the ultimate strategy for Nvidia is going to be coming out the other side," he said.

"However, I think directionally, just given the fact that Jensen was so crucial in selling the Nvidia story and being a very aggressive deal maker, it's likely that a lot of this creative deal-making and circular financing and equity and credit support is going to moderate," he added.

CoreWeave CEO Michael Intrator told CNBC Wednesday, before Huang's departure, that Nvidia "is not one person."

"There are thousands of people over there that interface with thousands of people at my company every day. They integrate on the technology. They integrate on the physical infrastructure. They integrate on the networking," he said in an interview with "Squawk on the Street." "They have been a great partner for us for years. They will continue to be a great partner for us."

Bob Lang, founder and chief strategist at Explosive Options, doesn't see any big changes in terms of Nvidia's relevance or its ability to finance for now. As CFO, Kress was involved in the financing deals, and the search for a permanent CEO is going to take some time, he noted.

The real issue is just how much money the company will want to lend for AI financing, rather than, say using its hundreds of billions in free cash flow for other purposes, like share buybacks, capex, or acquisitions.

"Big companies like Nvidia and Meta, they're holding all the cards," he said. "When they decide that the game is over, that's it — it's going to be curtains for some of these smaller companies."

— CNBC'S Kif Leswing and Isabel O'Brien contributed reporting.

#2764. Lakeland answers questions vs. St. Thomas Aquinas, other Open Division statements

All it took was one game to throw the Open Division into chaos.

Lakeland stunned top-seeded St. Thomas Aquinas, 27-24, in overtime on 13-yard touchdown pass from Kelin Hendrix to Joseph Howard Jr.

The same program that Aquinas shut out in the past two Class 5A state championship games went to Fort Lauderdale and forced football fans to rethink what they thought about the Open Division.

Forget St. Thomas Aquinas' quest for a national title. The Raiders now face a likely must-win situation when they travel to Venice for the second week of pool play.

And Lakeland? The Dreadnaughts have a chance to take firm control of Pool A when they travel to Chaminade-Madonna.

West Boca coach Dylan Potts said after the game that the Open Division is an emotional roller coaster. This is only the first week. We have four more weekends before crowning a state champion.

Here are quick takeaways from the first week of pool play:

Look, there were legitimate reasons to question Lakeland going into its game at Aquinas.

How would the team play in a big road game? The Dreadnaughts were the only Open Division squad that didn't play a regular season road game against a team ranked in the top 50 in the MaxPreps rankings.

Would Lakeland be able to move the ball after getting shut out two years in a row?

Those questions were answered on Lakeland's first drive when the Dreadnaughts marched down the field and scored. That touchdown changed everything.

This result wasn't a fluke. It was a statement. Lakeland was the better team. And now it's Aquinas that has questions to answer.

It's hard to imagine Raines having a better start against West Boca. The Vikings marched right down the field and scored a touchdown against a team that only allowed six touchdowns during the regular season.

After that? All West Boca. Junior athlete Terriel Harmon scored two touchdowns in the Bulls 28-7 win.

The win extends West Boca's postseason win streak to 11 games, which is now the longest in the state. For Raines, it ends the team's 25-game win streak.

It was an impressive performance for West Boca, which hosts a reeling Miami Central next week. Raines hosts No. 2 Columbus, which looks like a must-win game for the Vikings.

The biggest question for Venice was how would the Indiana secondary handle Chaminade's talented receivers? Venice didn't have a lot of answers.

Tromon Isaac Jr. caught a pair of touchdown passes in the Lions 35-0 win. The game had a running clock midway through the third quarter.

Venice had plenty of chances but had seven drives stall in Chaminade territory. That includes three interceptions thrown in the red zone.

Chaminade now hosts Lakeland with the winner being in pole position in Pool A. Venice heads back home for a must-win game against Aquinas.

For the second time in less than a month, Columbus easily dispatched Miami Central. This time the score was 40-3. It was 28-3 in less than three full quarters in the Greater Miami Athletic Conference final on Oct. 16.

Kai Moore threw a pair of touchdown passes for the Explorers. Columbus now makes its longest road trip of the season when it travels to Raines next week.

The biggest question is how will Miami Central respond? The Rockets have lost three games in the past four weeks, all by double digits. The team is already without starting quarterback James Perrone, who is out with an injury. Now Central has to go to West Boca and find a way to counter one of the state's premier defenses.

No. 1 St. Thomas Aquinas (0-1) at No. 8 Venice (0-1)

No. 5 Lakeland (1-0) at No. 4 Chaminade-Madonna (1-0)

No. 2 Columbus (1-0) at No. 7 Raines (0-1)

No. 6 Miami Central (0-1) at No. 3 West Boca (1-0)

Dejounte Murray of the New Orleans Pelicans reacts during a game against the Golden State Warriors in San Francisco, Monday, Nov. 9, 2026. Getty Images

"From what's going on INSIDE, this sounds like someone hit you to say this!! THE 'SABOTAGE' IS REAL!!" Murray wrote in the since-deleted post.

Murray, 30, reposted Charania's report on Friday afternoon.

At least one potential suitor has already emerged.

The Suns have expressed interest in acquiring Murray, per NBA insider Chris Haynes.

Murray has had a disastrous tenure with the franchise since he was traded by the Hawks in July 2024 for a package headlined by Dyson Daniels and two first-round picks.

Dejounte Murray and Derik Queen of the New Orleans Pelicans high-five during a game against the Brooklyn Nets in New Orleans, Wednesday, Nov. 11, 2026. NBAE via Getty Images

Daniels has since emerged as one of the best perimeter defenders in the NBA, while Murray has appeared in just 54 games in a Pelicans uniform, suffering a fractured hand in his New Orleans debut before tearing his Achilles in January 2025.

The Pelicans, who hired Jamahl Mosley as their head coach during the offseason, have lost four straight games, including a 51-point drubbing at the hands of the Warriors on Monday.

Murray is making $32.8 million this season and holds a $30.8 million player option for the 2027-28 campaign after signing a $114-plus million veteran extension in 2023.

#2762. Robert Griffin III is "expected to interview" for Baylor job

Former Baylor quarterback Robert Griffin III is interested in coaching his former college team. And his former college team is sufficiently interested in Griffin to talk to him about it.

Via Pete Nakos of On3.com, Griffin is “expected to interview” for the vacant position.

Other former players who have become college head coaches include Deion Sanders, Eddie George, and Mike Vick.

Getting an interview and getting the job are obviously two very different things. Still, Griffin has a foot in the door. Without that, it’s impossible to get an invitation to the table.

#2761. FAA flight debacle could disrupt Rams, Chargers, 49ers; NFL responds

The Rams have spent all week preparing for the Cardinals. Now, an unprecedented travel nightmare could force them to prepare for a road trip of an entirely different kind.

A nationwide ground stop issued by the Federal Aviation Administration on Friday afternoon has thrown weekend travel plans into chaos, potentially affecting several NFL teams scheduled to fly to their respective games Saturday.

U.S. Transportation Secretary Sean Duffy announced the shutdown about 1:15 p.m. PT, citing a communications issue affecting air traffic control centers across the country.

The grounding, reportedly only the second nationwide stoppage since the Sept. 11 terrorist attacks, has already disrupted college football travel. If it stretches into Saturday, the NFL could be next.

The Rams are scheduled to fly to Arizona on Saturday afternoon ahead of Sunday's 1:05 p.m. PT kickoff against the Cardinals in Glendale.

Los Angeles Rams defensive end Aaron Donald walks from the team bus. Los Angeles Times via Getty Imag

If the FAA's grounding order remains in effect throughout Saturday, Los Angeles could be forced to make the approximately 390-mile journey by charter bus, a six-to-seven-hour drive under favorable traffic conditions.

No contingency plan has been announced by the Rams, and there is no indication their scheduled departure will be affected.

Passengers check departure boards at Logan International Airport in East Boston, Mass., Friday, Nov. 13, 2026, during an FAA national ground stop. REUTERS

But with kickoff less than 48 hours away, the possibility of loading an entire NFL roster, coaching staff and equipment onto buses is hardly ideal.

FAA nationwide ground stop live updates: Agency lifts nationwide ground stop after flights halted for 4 hours

JetBlue cancels most remaining flights due to FAA ground stop — and predicts chaos won't end soon

FAA launched new AI 'SMART' tool to all air route traffic control centers just days before nationwide ground stop

College football flight chaos ensues after rare nationwide FAA ground stop

The 49ers are scheduled to fly to Dallas on Saturday, while the Chargers must travel across the country to Baltimore for Monday night's matchup against the Ravens.

The Raiders are home against Seattle, but the Seahawks could face travel complications.

Other teams have more manageable alternatives. Pittsburgh to Cincinnati, Minnesota to Green Bay and Buffalo to New Jersey (for Jets game) are all drives of roughly 300 miles or less.

NFL commissioner Roger Goodell arrives for an NFL game between the Pittsburgh Steelers and the New Orleans Saints in Saint-Denis, France, Sunday, Oct. 25, 2026. AFP via Getty Images

For teams traveling across multiple time zones, however, there is no convenient backup plan.

The NFL has not announced any schedule changes, and the situation remains entirely dependent on how quickly the FAA restores normal operations.

"It's too early. We could address the issue at the appropriate time, if necessary," said a league spokesman when asked about the ground stop.

For now, the Rams are preparing to fly. Whether they end up taking Interstate 10 instead could depend on what happens in the next 24 hours.

#2760. Is a bottom in for the Nasdaq-100? Charts to watch

(See the video above for Frank's complete chart breakdown.)

Is the Nasdaq-100's recent bounce a sign that the bottom is in? We're going to look at several charts to help answer that question.

First, let's remember how we got here. In September, the NDX broke out from a bullish cup-and-handle pattern, producing a 33,200-upside target. That breakout quickly failed, however, and gave way to a bearish pattern that broke down aggressively two weeks ago.

The decline brought the index relatively close to its 26,350 downside target before the current rebound began.

Zooming out, it's telling to see where the bounce originated. Three important forms of support converged in the 25,000-26,000 area:

This confluence of support helped encourage buying after a roughly 15% decline.

For perspective, we also see what larger drawdowns of 20%, 30%, 40%, and 50% would look like … a lot worse.

The following chart tracks NDX drawdowns from 52-week highs going back to just after the financial crisis. Only a handful of declines exceeded 20%, and three produced relatively quick reversals: late 2018, the Covid lows in 2020 and April 2025.

Historically, buying into declines of approximately 15%–25% has generally paid off, even when additional downside occurred first (yellow highlights).

The chart also highlights previous declines of 10%–16% in green, putting the current pullback into perspective.

The major exception was 2022, when the NDX eventually declined nearly 36% over approximately 10 months ... a much worse outcome, which many investors are concerned could happen again now.

That raises the key question: Are we experiencing another routine correction, or the early stages of a larger bear market?

One important clue will be how far this bounce progresses.

Any rally that fails to reclaim the previous highs could establish a significant lower high. Additional lower highs would provide stronger evidence of a developing, longer downtrend.

We saw this in March 2022, when that first sharp rebound effort failed to reach the previous high before a much more severe decline followed.

The bottom line: The current bounce is encouraging, particularly given where it originated and knowing how well buying prior 10-20% drawdowns has paid off.

From here, it's all about the follow through.  We don't need to see new highs right away, but any rally from here that's met with another wave of strong selling could reignite downside momentum.

Prior to starting CappThesis, he spent 25 years on Wall Street as an equity sales trader, technical analyst, research sales specialist and desk strategist.

#2759. John Harbaugh knew about the Jameis Winston "who let the dogs out?" cadence

Thursday night’s game between the Commanders and the Giants introduced a new wrinkle to the legend of Jameis Winston.

While attempting to get the Washington defense to jump offside on a fourth-and-one play in the fourth quarter, Jameis capped his pre-snap call with, “Who let the dogs out?”

The move invited curiosity as to whether Jameis was simply being Jameis and seizing on the moment, or whether coach John Harbaugh knew that the homage to the Baha Men was part of the plan.

“Yeah, I knew that,” Harbaugh told reporters. “That was part of the cadence, yeah. That’s part of the cadence. You know, teams do jump in those situations sometimes. So that was the idea.”

He’s right. It seems like at least once a week a defense jumps when it knows it shouldn’t. And it doesn’t take much to spark the desired reaction.

Last night, it’s possible that the specific cadence could have prompted one of the defenders to start laughing loud enough to tumble forward in his stance.

Regardless, it was fun. And that’s when football is at its best — when it’s fun.
